# Current Affairs

> Complete SSC CGL/CHSL study notes for **Current Affairs**. Generated from curated exam-focused content.

## National - Government Schemes (Launch dates, Ministries, Objectives, Beneficiaries) (National)

1.  Topic Introduction
    The given questions focus on Indian Polity and Governance, specifically covering major Government Schemes and Initiatives. This encompasses flagship welfare programs, financial inclusion models, healthcare policies, rural and urban development frameworks, and skill development programs launched by various central ministries. Understanding these schemes is a highly high-yielding and mandatory core segment of the General Awareness syllabus for the SSC CGL exam.

2.  Core Concepts Explained Simply  - Financial Inclusion: Initiatives like PMJDY aimed at providing universal access to banking facilities, financial literacy, and social security coverage to the unbanked population.
  - Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT): Mechanisms used in schemes like PM-KISAN and PMMVY to transfer subsidies or cash incentives directly into beneficiaries' bank accounts, eliminating intermediaries.
  - Social Security & Pensions: Contributory and non-contributory pension plans (like APY and PM-SYM) designed to provide financial stability to senior citizens and unorganized sector workers post-retirement.
  - Universal Healthcare Assurance: Welfare frameworks like PM-JAY providing robust insurance covers to vulnerable families for secondary and tertiary care hospitalizations.3.  Important Facts and Dates  - Launch Timelines: Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (August 28, 2014); Make in India (September 2014); Digital India (July 1, 2015); Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana - Urban (June 25, 2015); POSHAN Abhiyaan (March 8, 2018); Jal Jeevan Mission (2019).
  - Institutional Nodal Frameworks: PMMY offers Shishu (up to Rs. 50,000), Kishor (Rs. 50,000 to Rs. 5 Lakh), and Tarun (Rs. 5 Lakh to Rs. 10 Lakh) loans. Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana (SSY) allows account opening up to a maximum age of 10 years for a girl child.
  - Premium Rates under PMFBY: Kharif crops (2.0%), Rabi crops (1.5%), and annual commercial/horticultural crops (5.0%).4.  Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams  - Ministry Allocations: SSC regularly tests the specific central ministries executing key schemes (e.g., PMUY under Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas; PMKVY under Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship).
  - Tri-Ministerial Convergences: The Beti Bachao Beti Padhao (BBBP) initiative is a joint dedicated campaign run by the Ministry of Women and Child Development, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, and Ministry of Education.
  - Rural Mapping Technology: The SVAMITVA scheme uniquely utilizes advanced drone technology to map inhabited land parcels across rural India to issue property cards.5.  Differences Between Similar Terms  - Shishu vs. Kishor vs. Tarun: Under MUDRA loans, Shishu covers initial micro-loans up to Rs. 50,000; Kishor covers mid-tier credit up to Rs. 5 Lakh; Tarun represents the highest tier scaling up to Rs. 10 Lakh.
  - PM-KISAN vs. PM-SYM: PM-KISAN is a direct income support scheme for all landholding farmer families, whereas PM-SYM is a voluntary, contributory pension scheme tailored strictly for unorganized sector workers aged 18-40 years earning Rs. 15,000 or less monthly.
  - SBM-Gramin Phase-I vs. Phase-II: Phase-I focused explicitly on making rural areas Open Defecation Free (ODF) by constructing individual toilets, while Phase-II centers on sustaining ODF status and managing solid and liquid waste (ODF Plus).6.  Examples  - Traditional Crafts Preservation: The PM Vishwakarma Scheme serves as a classic model for providing credit, skill upgrades, and toolkit incentives exclusively to traditional artisans and craftsmen (weavers, blacksmiths, potters).
  - Regional Infrastructure Tailoring: The PM-DevINE scheme highlights targeted developmental intervention, funding infrastructure and social development specifically for the unique geographical challenges of the North-Eastern Region.
  - Multi-Modal Infrastructure Integration: PM Gati Shakti serves as a digital platform bringing 16 ministries together to plan and execute synchronized logistics and connectivity projects.7.  Previous-Year Exam Perspective  - SSC CGL exams constantly target the primary objectives, eligibility age criteria, target financial caps, and launch locations of central schemes (e.g., identifying Jhunjhunu, Rajasthan as the launch site for POSHAN Abhiyaan or Ranchi, Jharkhand for PM-JAY). Questions heavily prioritize financial inclusion targets, specific premium rates under crop insurances, and identifying which schemes replaced older versions (like PM POSHAN replacing the Mid-Day Meal Scheme).8.  Quick Revision Points and Mnemonics  - Financial Inclusion Pioneer: PMJDY = 2014 (The baseline foundation scheme).
  - MUDRA Categories: S-K-T (Shishu-Kishor-Tarun: Small, Kid, Teenager scale of loans).
  - APY Maximum Joining Age: 40 years (You can join till 40, payouts start at 60).
  - Crop Insurance Premium: Rabi = 1.5% (Rabi is shorter word, smaller value); Kharif = 2.0%; Commercial = 5.0% (Highest risk, highest premium).
  - Smart Cities Target: 100 Cities selected via challenge.

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **In which year was the Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY) launched to ensure financial inclusion across India?**
   - A) 2014
   - B) 2015
   - C) 2016
   - D) 2017
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: PMJDY was launched on August 28, 2014, by Prime Minister Narendra Modi as a national mission for financial inclusion.
Why others are wrong: 2015 saw the launch of Digital India and Mudra Yojana; 2016 was the launch year for Stand-Up India; 2017 featured the launch of Saubhagya Yojana.

2. **Which central ministry is responsible for the implementation of the Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY)?**
   - A) Ministry of Rural Development
   - B) Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
   - C) Ministry of Power
   - D) Ministry of Health and Family Welfare
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PMUY is implemented by the Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas to provide clean cooking fuel like LPG to rural and deprived households.
Why others are wrong: Ministry of Rural Development handles MGNREGA; Ministry of Power implements Saubhagya; Ministry of Health implements Ayushman Bharat.

3. **What is the primary objective of the Ayushman Bharat - Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PM-JAY)?**
   - A) To provide free primary education to children
   - B) To provide health cover of up to Rs. 5 lakh per family per year for secondary and tertiary care hospitalization
   - C) To provide pension to senior citizens
   - D) To offer crop insurance to farmers
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM-JAY is the world's largest health assurance scheme, aiming to provide a health cover of Rs. 5 lakh per family per year for secondary and tertiary care.
Why others are wrong: Free primary education is covered under Samagra Shiksha; Pensions are under schemes like APY; Crop insurance is covered under PMFBY.

4. **Who are the primary beneficiaries of the Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi (PM-KISAN) scheme?**
   - A) All landholding farmer families across the country
   - B) Landless agricultural laborers only
   - C) Urban street vendors
   - D) Micro-enterprise owners
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: PM-KISAN provides an income support of Rs. 6,000 per year in three equal installments to all landholding farmer families.
Why others are wrong: Landless laborers are supported through MGNREGA; Urban street vendors are targeted by PM SVANidhi; Micro-enterprise owners are covered under PMMY.

5. **What is the maximum age limit for a subscriber to join the Atal Pension Yojana (APY)?**
   - A) 35 years
   - B) 40 years
   - C) 50 years
   - D) 60 years
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: APY is open to all bank account holders aged between 18 and 40 years.
Why others are wrong: 35 and 50 years are incorrect eligibility boundaries; 60 years is the age at which pension payouts actually begin under the scheme.

6. **Which scheme was launched in 2015 with the vision of providing "Housing for All" in urban areas?**
   - A) Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana - Urban (PMAY-U)
   - B) Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana (DAY)
   - C) AMRUT
   - D) HRIDAY
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: PMAY-U was launched on June 25, 2015, by the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs to provide all-weather pucca houses to eligible urban beneficiaries.
Why others are wrong: DAY focuses on livelihoods; AMRUT targets urban infrastructure renewal; HRIDAY concentrated on heritage city development.

7. **The "Make in India" initiative, aimed at transforming India into a global design and manufacturing hub, was launched under which ministry?**
   - A) Ministry of Finance
   - B) Ministry of Corporate Affairs
   - C) Ministry of Commerce and Industry
   - D) Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Make in India was launched in September 2014 by the Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade under the Ministry of Commerce and Industry.
Why others are wrong: Ministry of Finance handles budgetary allocations; Ministry of Corporate Affairs handles company regulations; Ministry of Skill Development manages training initiatives.

8. **What was the main target achieved by the Swachh Bharat Mission - Gramin (SBM-G) during its Phase-I ending on October 2, 2019?**
   - A) 100% digital literacy in villages
   - B) Making all rural areas Open Defecation Free (ODF)
   - C) Providing piped water tap connections to all homes
   - D) Eliminating child labor in agriculture
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Phase-I of SBM-G aimed to make rural India Open Defecation Free by constructing household toilets by the 150th birth anniversary of Mahatma Gandhi.
Why others are wrong: Digital literacy is targeted by PMGDISHA; Piped water connections are under Jal Jeevan Mission; Eliminating child labor is managed through labor welfare acts.

9. **Which scheme restructured the erstwhile National Rural Livelihoods Mission into a focused self-help group movement?**
   - A) Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana - National Rural Livelihoods Mission (DAY-NRLM)
   - B) Pradhan Mantri Shram Yogi Maan-dhan (PM-SYM)
   - C) Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Grameen Kaushalya Yojana (DDU-GKY)
   - D) Mahatma Gandhi National Rural Employment Guarantee Act (MGNREGA)
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: DAY-NRLM seeks to alleviate rural poverty by organizing the rural poor into Self Help Groups (SHGs) and fostering sustainable livelihoods.
Why others are wrong: PM-SYM is a voluntary pension scheme; DDU-GKY is focused on rural youth placement-linked skills; MGNREGA is a demand-driven wage employment guarantee act.

10. **Under the Pradhan Mantri Mudra Yojana (PMMY), what is the maximum loan amount category classified as 'Tarun'?**
   - A) Up to Rs. 50,000
   - B) From Rs. 50,000 to Rs. 5 Lakh
   - C) From Rs. 5 Lakh to Rs. 10 Lakh
   - D) From Rs. 10 Lakh to Rs. 20 Lakh
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: PMMY offers three loan categories: Shishu (up to Rs. 50,000), Kishor (Rs. 50,000 to Rs. 5 Lakh), and Tarun (Rs. 5 Lakh to Rs. 10 Lakh).
Why others are wrong: Up to Rs. 50,000 is Shishu; Rs. 50,000 to Rs. 5 Lakh is Kishor; Loans above 10 Lakh do not fall under standard MUDRA categories.

11. **The Beti Bachao Beti Padhao (BBBP) initiative is a joint campaign run by three central ministries. Which of the following is NOT one of them?**
   - A) Ministry of Women and Child Development
   - B) Ministry of Health and Family Welfare
   - C) Ministry of Education
   - D) Ministry of Rural Development
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: BBBP is a tri-ministerial convergence effort of the Ministries of Women and Child Development, Health and Family Welfare, and Education (formerly Human Resource Development).
Why others are wrong: The other three choices are actively involved in designing and implementing the policy goals of BBBP.

12. **What is the maximum entry age limit for a girl child to open an account under Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana (SSY)?**
   - A) 5 years
   - B) 10 years
   - C) 12 years
   - D) 15 years
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: A Sukanya Samriddhi account can be opened in the name of a girl child from her birth till she attains the age of 10 years.
Why others are wrong: 5, 12, and 15 years are incorrect regulatory cutoffs established by the Ministry of Finance for opening this account.

13. **Under the Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana (PMFBY), what is the uniform premium rate payable by farmers for all Kharif crops?**
   - A) 1.5%
   - B) 2.0%
   - C) 5.0%
   - D) 10.0%
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PMFBY charges a low uniform premium of 2.0% for Kharif crops, 1.5% for Rabi crops, and 5.0% for commercial/horticultural crops.
Why others are wrong: 1.5% is for Rabi crops; 5.0% is for annual commercial and horticultural crops; 10.0% is not standard for any category under this scheme.

14. **Which flagship programme was launched in July 2015 to transform India into a digitally empowered society and knowledge economy?**
   - A) Startup India
   - B) Stand-Up India
   - C) Digital India
   - D) Skill India
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Digital India was launched on July 1, 2015, focusing on providing digital infrastructure, governance on demand, and digital empowerment.
Why others are wrong: Startup India supports entrepreneurship; Stand-Up India funds SC, ST, and women entrepreneurs; Skill India creates skilled workforces.

15. **The Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY) is a flagship scheme implemented by which ministry?**
   - A) Ministry of Labour and Employment
   - B) Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship
   - C) Ministry of Education
   - D) Ministry of Commerce and Industry
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PMKVY is implemented by the National Skill Development Corporation (NSDC) under the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship.
Why others are wrong: Ministry of Labour handles employment exchanges; Ministry of Education manages formal academics; Ministry of Commerce handles foreign and domestic trade policies.

16. **What is the primary objective of the Jal Jeevan Mission (JJM) launched in 2019?**
   - A) To clean the river Ganga
   - B) To provide Functional Household Tap Connections (FHTC) to every rural home
   - C) To build multi-purpose dams for electricity
   - D) To offer subsidised irrigation water to all farmers
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: JJM aims to provide safe and adequate drinking water through Functional Household Tap Connections (FHTC) to all households in rural India.
Why others are wrong: Cleaning Ganga is under Namami Gange; Dam construction falls under separate infrastructure policies; Irrigation is covered by PMKSY.

17. **Who among the following are eligible to benefit from the Pradhan Mantri Shram Yogi Maan-dhan (PM-SYM) scheme?**
   - A) Organized sector workers earning high salaries
   - B) Unorganized sector workers aged 18-40 with monthly income of Rs 15,000 or less
   - C) Central government employees only
   - D) Large scale industrial farmers
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM-SYM is a voluntary and contributory pension scheme meant for unorganized workers like street vendors, rickshaw pullers, and agricultural laborers within specified age and income thresholds.
Why others are wrong: Organized workers and central employees have separate provident funds/pension architectures; Large-scale farmers have independent income profiles.

18. **How many cities were originally selected to be developed as part of the Smart Cities Mission launched in 2015?**
   - A) 50
   - B) 100
   - C) 200
   - D) 500
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Smart Cities Mission was launched to promote sustainable and inclusive cities, with 100 cities selected through a nationwide challenge process.
Why others are wrong: 50 and 200 are incorrect metrics; 500 was the initial target city count for the AMRUT infrastructure scheme, not the Smart Cities challenge.

19. **The Stand-Up India Scheme aims to promote entrepreneurship specifically among which segments of the population?**
   - A) Only senior citizens
   - B) SC, ST, and Women entrepreneurs
   - C) Ex-servicemen only
   - D) Non-resident Indians (NRIs)
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Stand-Up India mandates scheduled commercial banks to extend loans between Rs. 10 lakh and Rs. 1 crore to at least one SC or ST borrower and one woman borrower per branch.
Why others are wrong: Senior citizens, ex-servicemen, and NRIs are not the targeted baseline categories under this affirmative economic scheme.

20. **What is the initial working capital loan amount provided to street vendors under the PM SVANidhi scheme?**
   - A) Up to Rs. 5,000
   - B) Up to Rs. 10,000
   - C) Up to Rs. 25,000
   - D) Up to Rs. 50,000
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM SVANidhi enables street vendors to access an initial working capital loan of up to Rs. 10,000 to resume businesses hit by the pandemic.
Why others are wrong: 5,000, 25,000, and 50,000 are incorrect amounts for the first tranche, though subsequent tranches can scale up upon timely repayment.

21. **The PM POSHAN scheme, which replaced the Mid-Day Meal Scheme, is aimed at providing cooked meals to children in which schools?**
   - A) Private international schools
   - B) Government and Government-aided schools
   - C) Only urban corporate schools
   - D) Higher education colleges
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM POSHAN provides one hot cooked meal to children in government and government-aided primary and upper primary classes.
Why others are wrong: Private, international, and corporate institutions do not receive this state welfare; Higher education falls outside early nutritional security goals.

22. **What does the abbreviation 'AMRUT' stand for in the context of urban transformation schemes?**
   - A) Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban Transformation
   - B) Awas Mission for Rural and Urban Tenants
   - C) Atal Movement for Rural and Urban Transport
   - D) Advanced Mission for River and Urban Technology
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: AMRUT stands for Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban Transformation, focusing on basic urban infrastructure like water supply, sewerage, and green spaces.
Why others are wrong: The other choices are completely fabricated expansions that do not correspond to any active government policy documentation.

23. **POSHAN Abhiyaan, India's flagship programme to improve nutritional outcomes for children, adolescents, pregnant women, and lactating mothers, was launched from which place?**
   - A) Jhunjhunu, Rajasthan
   - B) Gandhinagar, Gujarat
   - C) Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh
   - D) Ranchi, Jharkhand
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: POSHAN Abhiyaan (National Nutrition Mission) was launched by the Prime Minister from Jhunjhunu in Rajasthan on March 8, 2018.
Why others are wrong: Gandhinagar, Varanasi, and Ranchi have hosted launches of other central programs (like Ayushman Bharat from Ranchi), but not POSHAN Abhiyaan.

24. **What was the primary objective of the HRIDAY scheme launched by the Ministry of Urban Development?**
   - A) To improve cardiac healthcare facilities across states
   - B) Holistic development and preservation of heritage cities in India
   - C) Providing highway connectivity across borders
   - D) Developing modern electronics manufacturing clusters
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Heritage City Development and Augmentation Yojana (HRIDAY) aimed to preserve and revitalize the soul and unique character of heritage cities in India.
Why others are wrong: Cardiac health is addressed by national health missions; Highways are handled by Bharatmala; Electronics manufacturing is supported by PLI schemes.

25. **Under the Saansad Adarsh Gram Yojana (SAGY), what are Members of Parliament (MPs) responsible for?**
   - A) Allocating special cash directly to rural bank accounts
   - B) Adopting and developing physical and social infrastructure in a village
   - C) Constructing state high courts
   - D) Recruiting rural school teachers directly
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: SAGY focuses on social and cultural development, motivating communities to build model villages by leveraging existing schemes under the guidance of MPs.
Why others are wrong: MPs do not distribute direct cash support under this scheme; Court building and teacher recruitment belong strictly to state judiciary/administrative organs.

26. **The Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana (PMMVY) provides a cash incentive of Rs. 5,000 directly to whom?**
   - A) Unemployed youths looking for jobs
   - B) Pregnant Women and Lactating Mothers (PW\&LM)
   - C) Elderly women living alone
   - D) Girl students pursuing engineering
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PMMVY is a direct benefit transfer (DBT) scheme under which a cash incentive is provided to pregnant women for partial wage loss and to promote health-seeking behavior.
Why others are wrong: Unemployed youths are supported via training or unemployment benefits; Elderly women use pension schemes; Girl students receive distinct academic scholarships.

27. **What technology is utilized under the SVAMITVA scheme to map residential land parcels in rural areas?**
   - A) Satellite imagery alone
   - B) Drone technology
   - C) Manual chain surveying
   - D) Radar sensors underground
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: SVAMITVA uses drone technology to map inhabited land parcels in rural sectors, helping villagers obtain formal property validation documents.
Why others are wrong: Manual chains are slow and outdated; Satellite imagery lacks resolution for exact boundaries; Radar sensors are used for underground archaeological profiling.

28. **What is the primary focus of the PM Gati Shakti National Master Plan launched in 2021?**
   - A) Developing digital sports infrastructure
   - B) Integrated planning and coordinated implementation of infrastructure connectivity projects
   - C) Providing high-speed internet to every tribal block
   - D) Funding aerospace startups
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM Gati Shakti is a digital platform bringing 16 ministries together for integrated planning and synchronized execution of multi-modal connectivity infrastructure.
Why others are wrong: Sports, tribal internet (handled under BharatNet/PM-JANMAN), and aerospace startups have separate policy platforms unrelated to multi-modal logistics alignment.

29. **The PM-DevINE scheme was introduced specifically for the development of which geographical region of India?**
   - A) The coastal peninsular region
   - B) The North-Eastern Region
   - C) The Himalayan desert area
   - D) The central tribal belt
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM-DevINE stands for Prime Ministerâ€™s Development Initiative for North Eastern Region, funding infrastructure and social development projects in the NE states.
Why others are wrong: Peninsular, Himalayan, and central tribal areas have other targeted developmental sub-plans but do not fall under the purview of PM-DevINE.

30. **Which segment of workers is the PM Vishwakarma Scheme designed to support with credit, skill upgrades, and toolkit incentives?**
   - A) Software engineers and IT professionals
   - B) Traditional artisans and craftsmen
   - C) Gig workers and delivery executives
   - D) Commercial airline pilots
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: PM Vishwakarma provides comprehensive end-to-end support to traditional artisans and craftsmen who work with their hands and tools across various traditional trades.
Why others are wrong: IT professionals, gig workers, and commercial pilots belong to modern service sectors and are excluded from this traditional craft preservation framework.

## National - Bills & Acts (Key Bills Passed in Parliament) (National)

1.  Topic Introduction
    The given questions focus on Indian Polity and Governance, specifically covering Key Bills and Acts passed by the Parliament in recent times. This includes significant structural shifts in criminal law, digital data privacy, gender representation, administrative integration, environmental regulations, and constitutional amendments. Mastering these updates is crucial for the General Awareness section of the SSC CGL exam.

2.  Core Concepts Explained Simply  - Decriminalization & Ease of Doing Business: Shifting minor, technical, or procedural defaults from criminal penalties to civil monetary fines (as seen in the Jan Vishwas Act) to minimize harassment for businesses.
  - Data Fiduciary: Any person, company, or state entity that decides the purpose and means of processing personal data under the Digital Personal Data Protection Act.
  - Linear Projects: Infrastructure projects that are progressive and long in length, such as highways, pipelines, and railway lines, which receive specific exemptions under national security mandates.
  - Pre-Litigation Mediation: A structured, voluntary dispute resolution process that parties attempt before formally filing a lawsuit in a court of law to decrease judicial backlogs.3.  Important Facts and Dates  - Criminal Law Reform: The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS) officially replaced the colonial-era Indian Penal Code (IPC) of 1860.
  - Women's Reservation: The Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam was introduced as the 128th Constitutional Amendment Bill and enacted as the 106th Constitutional Amendment Act, providing 33% reservation for women.
  - Uniform GST Tier: A uniform 28% GST bracket was explicitly defined for actionable claims in online real-money gaming, casino betting, and horse racing.
  - Atomic Minerals Delisting: Six critical minerals, including Lithium, Titanium, and Beryllium, were removed from the atomic minerals list to allow private sector extraction.4.  Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams  - Repealed Historical Acts: SSC frequently tests the historical predecessors of modern laws (e.g., Press and Registration of Books Act, 1867 replaced by the Press and Registration of Periodicals Act, 2023).
  - High-Level Selection Committees: Composition of statutory selection panels, specifically the removal of the Chief Justice of India from the Election Commissioner selection panel in favor of a Union Cabinet Minister.
  - Special Nominations: Legislative quotas and powers of the Lieutenant Governor (e.g., nominating two Kashmiri Migrant members, including one woman, to the J\&K Assembly).5.  Differences Between Similar Terms  - Bill vs. Act: A Bill is a legislative proposal introduced in Parliament, whereas an Act is a Bill that has been passed by both Houses and received the assent of the President.
  - BNS vs. BNSS: The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS) replaces the substantive criminal law (IPC), while the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita (BNSS) replaces the procedural criminal law (CrPC).
  - Scheduled Castes (SC) vs. Scheduled Tribes (ST) Updates: Modifications to registries are state-specific; for example, the Mahra community was added to the SC list of Chhattisgarh, while separate tribal bills updated the ST classifications for communities like Dhanuwar.6.  Examples  - Unified Identity Proof: The digital birth certificate serves as a singular, legal document accepted for multiple identity verification pipelines like driving licenses, voter rolls, and school admissions.
  - Traditional Medicine Protection: Local AYUSH practitioners, Vaidyas, and Hakims are granted direct exemptions from the National Biodiversity Authority pipeline to utilize local biological resources easily.
  - Inter-Services Integration: Commanders-in-Chief of Inter-services Organisations holding direct disciplinary authority over personnel across the Army, Navy, and Air Force simultaneously.7.  Previous-Year Exam Perspective  - SSC CGL exams constantly target the precise numbers of recent Constitutional Amendments (e.g., matching the 106th Amendment to women's reservation). Questions frequently focus on the percentage thresholds mandated by new acts (like the 33% reservation rule), tax rates applied to specific modern sectors (28% GST on gaming), and the names of newly established tribal or national institutional setups.8.  Quick Revision Points and Mnemonics  - IPC Replacement: BNS = Nyaya (Justice replacing the Code of Punishment).
  - Women's Reservation: 128th Bill became the 106th Act (128 minus 106 highlights the legislative filtering process).
  - Dental Registry Reform: DCI dissolved to form NDC (Dental Council yields to the modern Commission structure).
  - Navigation Overhaul: Marine Aids to Navigation Act replaced the Lighthouse Act, 1927.

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **Which of the following Bills was passed by the Parliament to replace the colonial-era Indian Penal Code (IPC)?**
   - A) Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita
   - B) Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita
   - C) Bharatiya Sakshya Bill
   - D) Digital Personal Data Protection Bill
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS) was passed to repeal and replace the Indian Penal Code of 1860.
Why others are wrong: A replaces the CrPC; C replaces the Indian Evidence Act; D regulates digital data privacy.

2. **The Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023, establishes obligations for processing data for which entities?**
   - A) Only foreign companies operating outside India
   - B) Data Fiduciaries
   - C) Only state governments
   - D) Public sector banks exclusively
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Act mandates compliance rules for Data Fiduciaries who determine the purpose and means of processing personal data.
Why others are wrong: The obligations apply widely to entities handling Indian citizens' data, not restricted to foreign, state, or specific banking sectors.

3. **Which major Bill was enacted to grant 33% reservation for women in the Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assemblies?**
   - A) The Constitution (One Hundred and Sixth Amendment) Act
   - B) The Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam (128th Amendment Bill)
   - C) Both A and B
   - D) None of the above
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The 128th Constitutional Amendment Bill, popularly named the Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam, became the 106th Constitutional Amendment Act upon receiving presidential assent.
Why others are wrong: Both A and B correctly identify the legal and popular names of the exact same legislation.

4. **The Multi-State Co-operative Societies (Amendment) Bill aims to introduce which of the following reforms?**
   - A) Establishing a Co-operative Election Authority
   - B) Setting up a Co-operative Rehabilitation Fund
   - C) Improving governance and transparency in co-operatives
   - D) All of the above
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: The Bill introduces comprehensive governance measures including an election authority, a fund for reviving sick co-operatives, and enhanced transparency metrics.
Why others are wrong: Options A, B, and C are all specific individual pillars included in this overarching legislation.

5. **Under the Forest (Conservation) Amendment Act, 2023, which type of land is exempted from the purview of the original 1980 Act?**
   - A) All private agricultural lands without restrictions
   - B) Land situated within 100 km along international borders for strategic linear projects
   - C) Core areas of national tiger reserves
   - D) Deep sea marine conservation zones
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment explicitly exempts strategic national security infrastructure projects located within 100 km of international borders from forest clearance requirements.
Why others are wrong: Private agricultural lands are not universally exempted; tiger reserves and marine zones see stricter protections rather than blanket exemptions.

6. **The Inter-Services Organisations (Command, Control and Discipline) Bill empower whom with disciplinary powers?**
   - A) The Ministry of Home Affairs exclusively
   - B) The Supreme Court of India
   - C) Commanders-in-Chief and Officers-in-Command of Inter-services Organisations
   - D) Local municipal commissioners
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The legislation empowers heads of Inter-services Organisations to exercise full administrative and disciplinary control over all personnel serving under them, regardless of their parent service branch (Army, Navy, or Air Force).
Why others are wrong: The Bill focuses on military operational integration, bypassing civilian ministries or courts for day-to-day command discipline.

7. **The Cinematograph (Amendment) Act, 2023, introduces strict provisions targeting which illegal activity?**
   - A) Foreign funding of regional films
   - B) Unauthorized recording and piracy of films
   - C) Overpricing of movie theater snacks
   - D) Celebrity tax evasion
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment explicitly prohibits unauthorized recording and transmission of copyrighted cinematographic content to curb rampant film piracy.
Why others are wrong: It does not address financial distribution regulations, cinema hall catering costs, or personal income tax compliance.

8. **The Registration of Births and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023, allows the digital birth certificate to be used as a single document for:**
   - A) Admission to educational institutions
   - B) Issuance of driving licenses
   - C) Preparation of voter lists
   - D) All of the above
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: The updated Act legalizes a single digital birth certificate to streamline identity proofing across multiple services like school admissions, driving licenses, passports, and voter rolls.
Why others are wrong: Selecting any singular option excludes the other valid applications legalized simultaneously under the unified mandate.

9. **Which regulatory bodyâ€™s structure was updated via the Jan Vishwas (Amendment of Provisions) Act, 2023, to decriminalize minor offenses?**
   - A) Decrimilalization across 42 acts managed by various ministries
   - B) Reserve Bank of India exclusively
   - C) Central Board of Direct Taxes only
   - D) ISRO space commercial wings
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Jan Vishwas Act systematically decriminalized minor, technical offenses across 42 separate legislations to boost ease of doing business.
Why others are wrong: The scope is wide-ranging across various corporate and public sectors, rather than unique to one banking, tax, or space agency.

10. **The Coastal Aquaculture Authority (Amendment) Bill, 2023, explicitly legalizes which forms of environmentally friendly aquaculture?**
   - A) Deep-sea commercial oil drilling
   - B) Hatcheries, broodstock multiplication centers, and nucleus breeding centers
   - C) Industrial plastic manufacturing near coasts
   - D) Unregulated mangrove deforestation for shrimp farming
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment recognizes newer, safer modern techniques like advanced hatcheries and specific genetic breeding installations to support sustainable coastal farming.
Why others are wrong: Industrial drilling, plastic generation, and mangrove destruction are ecologically hazardous and heavily prohibited.

11. **The National Research Foundation (Anusandhan) Bill, 2023, replaces which existing body?**
   - A) Science and Engineering Research Board (SERB)
   - B) Council of Scientific & Industrial Research (CSIR)
   - C) Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR)
   - D) Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO)
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Anusandhan National Research Foundation (NRF) absorbed and replaced the Science and Engineering Research Board (SERB) to provide strategic direction for scientific research.
Why others are wrong: CSIR, ICMR, and DRDO remain active sovereign bodies with independent structural mandates.

12. **The Central Goods and Services Tax (Amendment) Bill, 2023, specifically defines and imposes a 28% integrated tax on:**
   - A) Essential food grains
   - B) Online gaming, casinos, and horse racing
   - C) Solar panels and wind turbines
   - D) Educational textbooks
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Parliament amended the GST laws to impose a uniform 28% tax bracket on actionable claims associated with online real-money gaming, casino betting, and horse racing.
Why others are wrong: Food grains and textbooks are exempted or heavily subsidized; green energy technologies sit in significantly lower, incentivized tax tiers.

13. **The Advocates (Amendment) Act, 2023, was passed to repeal obsolete provisions and consolidate the law under a single act, targeting which practice?**
   - A) Prohibiting foreign lawyers from visiting India
   - B) Eradicating the menace of touts in courts
   - C) Allowing artificial intelligence to completely replace judges
   - D) Abolishing the Bar Council of India
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment incorporates sections of the obsolete Legal Practitioners Act into the Advocates Act, 1961, focus-targeting the suppression of touts who exploit litigants.
Why others are wrong: It does not bar international legal consultations, does not introduce judicial AI replacements, and fully retains the Bar Council of India.

14. **The Press and Registration of Periodicals Bill, 2023, replaces which colonial-era legislation?**
   - A) The Press and Registration of Books Act, 1867
   - B) The Vernacular Press Act, 1878
   - C) The Rowlatt Act, 1919
   - D) The Official Secrets Act, 1923
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The new Bill modernizes the registration processes for periodicals, officially replacing the archaic Press and Registration of Books Act, 1867.
Why others are wrong: The Vernacular Press Act was repealed in the 19th century; Rowlatt was a sedition law; the Official Secrets Act governs espionage and state secrets.

15. **The Post Office Bill, 2023, empowers designated officers to intercept postal articles on which specific grounds?**
   - A) Commercial competition tracking
   - B) Public safety, emergency, or security of the State
   - C) Personal amusement of postal employees
   - D) Request of private marketing agencies
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Interception rights are strictly confined to critical circumstances concerning state security, friendly relations with foreign nations, public order, or emergencies.
Why others are wrong: Corporate spying, personal nosiness, and marketing data harvesting violate fundamental privacy laws and are strictly illegal.

16. **The Chief Election Commissioner and other Election Commissioners (Appointment, Conditions of Service and Term of Office) Act, 2023, sets up a selection committee consisting of:**
   - A) Prime Minister, Leader of Opposition/Single Largest Opposition Party Leader, and a designated Union Cabinet Minister
   - B) Chief Justice of India, Prime Minister, and President
   - C) Only the Union Home Minister
   - D) A panel of state Chief Ministers selected alphabetically
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The new Act prescribes a three-member Selection Committee headed by the Prime Minister, alongside the Leader of the Opposition and a Cabinet Minister nominated by the PM.
Why others are wrong: The Chief Justice of India was excluded from the final panel structure legislated by Parliament; individual home ministers or alphabetical chief ministers do not compose this statutory body.

17. **The Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2023, provides for the nomination of how many members from the Kashmiri Migrant community to the legislative assembly?**
   - A) Zero
   - B) Two
   - C) Ten
   - D) Fifty
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Lieutenant Governor is empowered to nominate up to two members from the Kashmiri Migrant community, one of whom must be a woman.
Why others are wrong: Zero ignores the newly created reservation; ten and fifty are disproportionately high and factually incorrect representation counts under the Act.

18. **What is the primary purpose of the Telecommunications Bill, 2023?**
   - A) To nationalize all private mobile network operators
   - B) To amend and consolidate the law relating to development, expansion, and operation of telecommunication services
   - C) To ban the usage of smartphones by minors
   - D) To eliminate the concept of satellite-based internet
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Telecommunications Bill, 2023, replaces century-old laws like the Indian Telegraph Act, 1885, to create a modern regulatory framework for telecom networks and spectrum assignment.
Why others are wrong: It maintains a thriving private market, permits smartphone use safely, and actively creates frameworks for satellite internet spectrum allocation.

19. **The Mines and Minerals (Development and Regulation) Amendment Act, 2023, introduces deep-seated structural shifts by removing which group from atomic minerals list?**
   - A) Granite and marble
   - B) Critical minerals like Lithium, Beryllium, and Titanium
   - C) Coal and Petroleum products exclusively
   - D) Common river bed sand
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment delisted six critical minerals (including Lithium) from the atomic minerals list, enabling commercial private entities to mine them and boost domestic clean energy production.
Why others are wrong: Granite, marble, and sand were never categorized as dangerous atomic minerals; coal and petroleum are managed under alternative fossil fuel frameworks.

20. **The Constitution (Scheduled Castes) Order (Amendment) Act, 2023, added which state's specific community to the Scheduled Castes list?**
   - A) Mahar community of Maharashtra
   - B) Mahra and Manjhi communities in Chhattisgarh
   - C) Chamar community of Uttar Pradesh
   - D) Toda tribe of Tamil Nadu
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Act specifically included the Mahra, Mahara, and Manjhi communities of Chhattisgarh into the stateâ€™s Scheduled Castes registry.
Why others are wrong: The communities in Maharashtra, UP, and Tamil Nadu were either already classified previously or represent separate Scheduled Tribe categories entirely.

21. **The National Dental Commission Act, 2023, replaces which archaic statutory body?**
   - A) Medical Council of India (MCI)
   - B) Dental Council of India (DCI)
   - C) Pharmacy Council of India (PCI)
   - D) Indian Nursing Council (INC)
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Act dissolves the long-standing Dental Council of India (DCI) to introduce a modern National Dental Commission (NDC) structure mirroring the National Medical Commission.
Why others are wrong: MCI was already replaced by the NMC; PCI regulates pharmacists; INC regulates nursing professionals.

22. **The Offshore Areas Mineral (Development and Regulation) Amendment Act, 2023, introduces what transparent mechanism for allocating production leases?**
   - A) Discretionary allocation by local state governors
   - B) First-come, first-served application lines
   - C) Competitive bidding through e-auction
   - D) Direct lottery draw systems
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The amendment mandates competitive e-auctions to ensure transparency and non-discrimination in granting exploration licenses and production leases in India's territorial offshore waters.
Why others are wrong: Discretionary powers, lottery systems, and first-come-first-served queues are notoriously prone to corruption and lack commercial efficiency.

23. **The Central Universities (Amendment) Act, 2023, provides for the establishment of a new tribal university in which state?**
   - A) Telangana
   - B) Kerala
   - C) Punjab
   - D) Goa
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Act establishes the Sarakka Central Tribal University in the State of Telangana to facilitate higher education access for local tribal populations.
Why others are wrong: Kerala, Punjab, and Goa were not designated hosts for this specific federal institutional establishment under the 2023 university amendment.

24. **The Marine Aids to Navigation Act aimed to modernize maritime safety infrastructure, replacing which colonial statute?**
   - A) The Lighthouse Act, 1927
   - B) The Pirates Suppression Act, 1800
   - C) The East India Company Port Rules
   - D) The Suez Canal Navigation Pact
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Act updates the management of lighthouses and advanced vessel traffic services, formally repealing the archaic Lighthouse Act, 1927.
Why others are wrong: Anti-piracy rules, East India company relics, and international canal treaties are separate frameworks outside Indian domestic lighthouse upkeep.

25. **The National Nursing and Midwifery Commission Act, 2023, aims to bring parity, transparency, and regulation by establishing:**
   - A) National, Regional, and State-level Nursing Councils under a unified commission
   - B) A single automated AI platform with zero human oversight
   - C) A completely unregulated free-market model for nursing degrees
   - D) A merger of all dental and nursing operations into a single degree
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Act institutes the National Nursing and Midwifery Commission to systematically regulate education, professionals, and clinical standards across multiple operational tiers.
Why others are wrong: It enforces robust regulatory oversight rather than leaving it to AI, completely unregulated free markets, or illogical merges with dentistry.

26. **The Biological Diversity (Amendment) Act, 2023, exempts which group of practitioners from seeking prior approval from the National Biodiversity Authority for accessing biological resources?**
   - A) Foreign pharmaceutical conglomerates
   - B) Local Ayush practitioners, Vaidyas, and Hakims
   - C) Commercial plastic manufacturing units
   - D) International deep-sea fishing trawlers
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The amendment encourages traditional Indian medicine systems by exempting registered local AYUSH doctors and folk healers from tedious bureaucratic clearance pipelines.
Why others are wrong: Transnational pharmaceutical corporations, plastic makers, and large international trawlers still require exhaustive scrutiny to prevent biopiracy.

27. **The Constitution (Scheduled Tribes) Order (Fifth Amendment) Act, 2023, included several communities from which state into the ST category?**
   - A) Chhattisgarh
   - B) Rajasthan
   - C) Gujarat
   - D) Haryana
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: This specific fifth amendment accommodated Dhanuhar, Dhanuwar, Kisan, Saunra, Saonra, and Binjhia communities into the ST list of Chhattisgarh.
Why others are wrong: While other states periodically update schedules, this exact legislative amendment package target-focused Chhattisgarh's demographics.

28. **What primary objective does the Mediation Act, 2023, seek to achieve?**
   - A) Making all courtroom litigation illegal in India
   - B) Promoting, institutionalizing, and streamlining pre-litigation mediation and settlements
   - C) Forcing married couples to divorce without counseling
   - D) Replacing the police force with private arbiters
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Mediation Act, 2023, cuts down judicial backlogs by formalizing a legal framework for voluntary and structured pre-litigation mediation processes.
Why others are wrong: It complements, rather than outlaws, the court system; it does not mandate absolute divorce protocols; and it bears no impact on the executive authority of the police force.

29. **The Bharatiya Sakshya Bill was passed by Parliament to replace which of the following?**
   - A) The Indian Evidence Act, 1872
   - B) The Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973
   - C) The Indian Penal Code, 1860
   - D) The Arms Act, 1959
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Bharatiya Sakshya Adhiniyam modernizes evidentiary rules (especially regarding electronic documentation), directly replacing the Indian Evidence Act, 1872.
Why others are wrong: Criminal Procedure is replaced by the BNSS; IPC is replaced by the BNS; the Arms Act remains distinct.

30. **The High Court and Supreme Court Judges (Salaries and Conditions of Service) Amendment Acts primarily seek to clarify:**
   - A) Complete removal of income taxes for judges
   - B) The precise date from which retired judges receive enhanced pension allocations based on age brackets
   - C) The allotment of separate sovereign micro-nations to each judge
   - D) Allowing judges to double as active political party presidents
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The adjustments clarify arithmetic calculations regarding when senior retired judges transition into higher pension brackets as they cross critical age milestones (such as turning 80, 85, etc.).
Why others are wrong: Judges pay standard taxes, do not receive private micro-nations, and are constitutionally barred from holding active political offices while serving the judiciary.

## National - Cabinet Decisions (Major policy decisions) (National)

1.  Topic Introduction
    The given questions focus on Indian Polity, specifically covering the Union Cabinet, the Council of Ministers, and the Cabinet Secretariat. This encompasses the structure of the executive branch in India's parliamentary democracy, the constitutional limits placed on executive size, and the operational machinery that executes governmental policies. These topics represent a crucial core segment of the General Awareness (Polity) syllabus for the SSC CGL exam.

2.  Core Concepts Explained Simply  - The Cabinet vs. Council of Ministers: The Council of Ministers is a large, formal body comprising all categories of ministers (Cabinet, Ministers of State, and Deputy Ministers). The Cabinet is an inner core group of senior ministers who handle major portfolios and act as the supreme policy-formulating body.
  - Collective Responsibility: Mandated by Article 75(3), it means the entire Council of Ministers is collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha. If the house loses confidence in the government, all ministers must resign together.
  - Cabinet Committees: These are extra-constitutional, functional bodies created through the Rules of Business to reduce the workload of the Cabinet. They are usually chaired by the Prime Minister, though exceptions like Accommodation exist.3.  Important Facts and Dates  - Constitutional Mention: The term 'Cabinet' was explicitly inserted into the text of the Constitution only via Article 352 by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act of 1978.
  - Size Limitation: The 91st Constitutional Amendment Act of 2003 capped the total size of the Council of Ministers at 15% of the total strength of the Lok Sabha.
  - Conduct of Business: Article 77 outlines that all executive actions of the Government of India are formally taken in the name of the President.4.  Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams  - Constitutional Articles: SSC frequently targets Article 74 (Council of Ministers to aid and advise the President), Article 75 (Appointment and responsibility of ministers), Article 76 (Attorney General), and Article 78 (Duties of the Prime Minister).
  - Highest Bureaucratic Post: The Cabinet Secretary is the senior-most civil servant in India and serves as the administrative head of the Cabinet Secretariat.
  - Parliamentary Devices: The Passing of a Vote of No-Confidence in the Lok Sabha immediately dissolves the sitting Council of Ministers.5.  Differences Between Similar Terms  - Article 74 vs. Article 75: Article 74 establishes the existence of the Council of Ministers to advise the President, whereas Article 75 outlines their specific appointment, tenure, and collective accountability.
  - Cabinet Secretary vs. Principal Secretary to the PM: The Cabinet Secretary heads the permanent Cabinet Secretariat ensuring bureaucratic coordination across all ministries, while the Principal Secretary leads the PMO, providing direct personal assistance to the Prime Minister.
  - Statutory vs. Extra-Constitutional: Statutory bodies are established by an explicit Act of Parliament, while Cabinet Committees are extra-constitutional because they emerge out of executive business rules rather than specific legislative or constitutional acts.6.  Examples  - Enforcing Solidarity: If a minister fundamentally disagrees with an executive decision (such as historical precedents like Dr. B.R. Ambedkar), they must resign rather than publicly criticize or vote against it in Parliament.
  - Super-Cabinet: The Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs is considered a 'Super-Cabinet' because it covers almost all internal and foreign policy affairs.
  - Written Advice Mandate: The President cannot proclaim a National Emergency under Article 352 based solely on the oral advice of the Prime Minister; a formal written decision from the entire Union Cabinet is required.7.  Previous-Year Exam Perspective  - SSC CGL questions heavily focus on exact matching of articles, identifying who chairs specific committees, and distinguishing which bodies are constitutional versus extra-constitutional. Questions often test exceptionsâ€”such as noting that the Cabinet Committee on Accommodation is historically chaired by the Home Minister rather than the Prime Minister, or recognizing that the budget requires Cabinet approval prior to parliamentary presentation.8.  Quick Revision Points and Mnemonics  - Cabinet Reference: Article 352 = Only place 'Cabinet' appears (Think: 3+5+2 = 10, a perfect score for the elite inner team).
  - Size Limit: 91st Amendment = 15% limit (9-1 = 8, close to the maximum size index implemented in 2003).
  - Bureaucratic Peak: Highest Civil Servant = Cabinet Secretary.
  - Responsibility Target: Council of Ministers -\> Accountable ONLY to Lok Sabha (Popular House).

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **Which Article of the Indian Constitution explicitly mentions the term 'Cabinet'?**
   - A) Article 74
   - B) Article 75
   - C) Article 352
   - D) Article 78
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The word 'Cabinet' was inserted into Article 352 of the Indian Constitution by the 44th Constitutional Amendment Act of 1978. Why others are wrong: Article 74 and 75 speak only of the Council of Ministers; Article 78 describes the duties of the Prime Minister in furnishing information to the President.

2. **Who heads the Union Cabinet in India?**
   - A) The President
   - B) The Vice-President
   - C) The Speaker of Lok Sabha
   - D) The Prime Minister
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: The Prime Minister is the ex-officio head of the Union Cabinet and presides over its meetings. Why others are wrong: The President is the executive head of the State but not the head of the Cabinet; the Vice-President heads the Rajya Sabha; the Speaker heads the Lok Sabha.

3. **By which Constitutional Amendment Act was the size of the Council of Ministers limited to 15% of the total strength of the Lok Sabha?**
   - A) 42nd Amendment Act
   - B) 44th Amendment Act
   - C) 91st Amendment Act
   - D) 86th Amendment Act
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The 91st Constitutional Amendment Act of 2003 restricted the size of the Council of Ministers, including the Prime Minister, to 15% of the Lok Sabha's total strength. Why others are wrong: The 42nd and 44th Amendments dealt with emergency provisions and fundamental rights; the 86th Amendment made education a fundamental right.

4. **Which body acts as the supreme policy-making body in the Central Government of India?**
   - A) Planning Commission
   - B) Cabinet
   - C) Parliament
   - D) Finance Commission
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet is the supreme policy-formulating and decision-making body of the Central Government. Why others are wrong: The Planning Commission (now NITI Aayog) is an advisory body; Parliament is a legislative body that passes laws; the Finance Commission is a constitutional body for fiscal devolution.

5. **The Council of Ministers is collectively responsible to which organ of the state?**
   - A) The President
   - B) The Rajya Sabha
   - C) The Lok Sabha
   - D) The Supreme Court
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: According to Article 75(3), the Council of Ministers is collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha (House of the People). Why others are wrong: Ministers hold office during the pleasure of the President individually, not collectively; they are not collectively accountable to the Rajya Sabha or the judiciary.

6. **Which Cabinet Committee is often described as a 'Super-Cabinet'?**
   - A) Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs
   - B) Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs
   - C) Cabinet Committee on Security
   - D) Cabinet Committee on Parliamentary Affairs
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs deals with all domestic and foreign policy issues and is considered the most powerful committee. Why others are wrong: Economic Affairs deals with financial matters; Security deals with defense and law and order; Parliamentary Affairs looks after government business in Parliament.

7. **Who presides over the meetings of the Cabinet Committees in India when the Prime Minister is a member?**
   - A) The Cabinet Secretary
   - B) The Home Minister
   - C) The Prime Minister
   - D) The Senior-most Minister
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Prime Minister heads and presides over all Cabinet Committees of which he or she is a member. Why others are wrong: The Cabinet Secretary is a civil servant and cannot preside; the Home Minister or senior ministers only preside if the Prime Minister is not a member.

8. **Which Cabinet Committee is currently NOT chaired by the Prime Minister?**
   - A) Cabinet Committee on Accommodation
   - B) Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs
   - C) Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs
   - D) Cabinet Committee on Security
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet Committee on Accommodation is usually chaired by the Union Home Minister, not the Prime Minister. Why others are wrong: The Committees on Economic Affairs, Political Affairs, and Security are strictly chaired by the Prime Minister.

9. **Who provides secretarial assistance to the Union Cabinet meetings?**
   - A) Prime Minister's Office (PMO)
   - B) Cabinet Secretariat
   - C) Ministry of Home Affairs
   - D) Department of Legal Affairs
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet Secretariat operates directly under the Prime Minister and ensures secretarial assistance to the Cabinet and its committees. Why others are wrong: The PMO provides personal staff support to the PM; the Ministry of Home Affairs and Department of Legal Affairs are independent ministerial departments.

10. **What type of body are Cabinet Committees in India?**
   - A) Constitutional bodies
   - B) Statutory bodies
   - C) Extra-constitutional bodies
   - D) Judicial bodies
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Cabinet Committees are extra-constitutional bodies created through the Rules of Business and are not explicitly mentioned in the text of the Constitution. Why others are wrong: Constitutional bodies are created by the text itself; statutory bodies are set up by an Act of Parliament; judicial bodies act as courts.

11. **Who is the administrative head of the Cabinet Secretariat?**
   - A) Principal Secretary to the PM
   - B) Home Secretary
   - C) Cabinet Secretary
   - D) Union Minister of State
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet Secretary is the top bureaucratic official and the administrative head of the Cabinet Secretariat. Why others are wrong: The Principal Secretary leads the PMO; the Home Secretary leads the Ministry of Home Affairs; a Minister of State is a political executive.

12. **Which of the following ranks is the highest among civil servants in the Government of India?**
   - A) Foreign Secretary
   - B) Cabinet Secretary
   - C) Defense Secretary
   - D) Finance Secretary
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet Secretary is the senior-most civil servant in the Government of India's official hierarchy. Why others are wrong: The Foreign, Defense, and Finance Secretaries report through their respective ministries and are junior in ranking to the Cabinet Secretary.

13. **What is the minimum number of Cabinet Ministers required to recommend a proclamation of National Emergency under Article 352?**
   - A) The Prime Minister alone
   - B) A majority of the entire Council of Ministers
   - C) The entire Cabinet in writing
   - D) Two-thirds of the Parliament
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The 44th Amendment mandated that the President can declare a National Emergency only after receiving a written communication from the Union Cabinet. Why others are wrong: The PM alone cannot recommend it anymore; the larger Council of Ministers is not required; Parliament approves the proclamation after it is issued.

14. **What differentiates the 'Cabinet' from the 'Council of Ministers'?**
   - A) The Cabinet includes Ministers of State
   - B) The Cabinet is a smaller body consisting of only Cabinet Ministers
   - C) The Council of Ministers meets more frequently than the Cabinet
   - D) The Cabinet has no constitutional recognition
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet is an inner core group consisting of 15-20 senior ministers, whereas the Council of Ministers is a larger body of 60-80 ministers. Why others are wrong: Ministers of State are part of the Council but generally not the Cabinet; the Cabinet meets regularly while the full Council rarely meets; the Cabinet has recognition under Article 352.

15. **Who implements the decisions taken by the Union Cabinet?**
   - A) The President of India
   - B) The Supreme Court
   - C) The respective Ministries and Departments
   - D) The Parliament Secretariat
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Once the Cabinet takes a major policy decision, the administrative ministries and departments are responsible for its execution. Why others are wrong: The President formally assents to laws but doesn't implement policy; the Supreme Court reviews legality; the Parliament Secretariat handles legislative administration.

16. **Decisions taken by the Union Cabinet are binding on which group of individuals?**
   - A) All citizens automatically
   - B) All members of the Council of Ministers
   - C) Only the Opposition members
   - D) The Judiciary
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Under the principle of collective responsibility, any decision taken by the Cabinet is binding on all ministers, even if they disagreed during the meeting. Why others are wrong: Citizens are bound by laws passed, not internal executive decisions; it does not bind the political opposition or the independent judiciary.

17. **If a Minister disagrees fundamentally with a major policy decision taken by the Cabinet, what is the expected constitutional recourse?**
   - A) File a lawsuit in the Supreme Court
   - B) Resign from the Council of Ministers
   - C) Vote against the government in Lok Sabha
   - D) Ignore the implementation of the policy
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: If a minister cannot defend or accept a Cabinet decision, they must resign from the ministry, as seen historically with Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and others. Why others are wrong: Ministers cannot sue their own government, vote against it while remaining in power, or intentionally sabotage policy implementation.

18. **Which body approves the presentation of the Union Budget before it is introduced in the Parliament?**
   - A) NITI Aayog
   - B) The Union Cabinet
   - C) Estimates Committee
   - D) Public Accounts Committee
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Union Budget is a major policy document that requires formal approval from the Union Cabinet before being introduced by the Finance Minister. Why others are wrong: NITI Aayog prepares long-term plans; Estimates and Public Accounts are parliamentary committees that scrutinize expenditures post-introduction or post-spending.

19. **Under which rules does the Union Cabinet conduct its day-to-day business and meetings?**
   - A) Code of Civil Procedure
   - B) Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules
   - C) Parliamentary Procedure Rules
   - D) Indian Penal Code
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The transaction of executive business is governed by the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules and Transaction of Business Rules, framed under Article 77. Why others are wrong: Code of Civil Procedure applies to civil courts; Parliamentary rules apply inside the houses; Indian Penal Code deals with substantive criminal law.

20. **Who determines the agenda for a Union Cabinet meeting?**
   - A) The President of India
   - B) The Cabinet Secretary in consultation with the Prime Minister
   - C) The Speaker of Lok Sabha
   - D) The Chief Justice of India
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The agenda for Cabinet meetings is finalized by the Cabinet Secretary under the direct leadership and approval of the Prime Minister. Why others are wrong: The President, Speaker, and Chief Justice do not participate in or dictate the internal executive functioning of the government.

21. **Which category of ministers is allowed to attend Union Cabinet meetings regularly?**
   - A) Deputy Ministers
   - B) Ministers of State with independent charge
   - C) Cabinet Ministers
   - D) Parliamentary Secretaries
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Only Cabinet-rank ministers have the right to attend all Cabinet meetings. Why others are wrong: Deputy Ministers and Ministers of State only attend if specifically invited when a matter concerning their department is discussed.

22. **Which of the following functions does the Cabinet NOT perform?**
   - A) Deciding on foreign treaties and pacts
   - B) Resolving disputes between states over water resources
   - C) Approving ordinances to be promulgated by the President
   - D) Finalizing the government's legislative agenda
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Inter-state water disputes are resolved through tribunals set up under statutory laws, not directly by Cabinet decisions. Why others are wrong: The Cabinet actively decides on foreign policy, recommends ordinances, and structures the legislative agenda.

23. **Cabinet decisions are characterized by which operational principle?**
   - A) Public Transparency during debate
   - B) Rule of Secrecy
   - C) Judicial Review before implementation
   - D) Majority vote by secret ballot
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The proceedings of the Cabinet are strictly confidential and protected by the oath of secrecy taken by the ministers. Why others are wrong: Debates are not made public; decisions are not reviewed by judges beforehand; decisions are reached by consensus rather than a formal ballot.

24. **What happens if a vote of no-confidence is passed against the Prime Minister in the Lok Sabha?**
   - A) Only the Prime Minister resigns
   - B) The Cabinet goes to the Rajya Sabha
   - C) The entire Council of Ministers must resign
   - D) A national emergency is automatically declared
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: A vote of no-confidence sinks the entire ministry, forcing the Prime Minister and the entire Council of Ministers to step down together. Why others are wrong: The PM cannot remain alone; the Rajya Sabha has no say in the survival of the executive; no emergency is triggered by political transitions.

25. **Who appoints the Cabinet Ministers of the Government of India?**
   - A) The Prime Minister
   - B) The President on the advice of the Prime Minister
   - C) The Parliament through elections
   - D) The Chief Justice of India
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Under Article 75(1), the President appoints all ministers, but this power can only be exercised on the formal advice of the Prime Minister. Why others are wrong: The PM selects but does not formally appoint; Parliament elects members but not ministers; the judiciary is completely separate.

26. **Which constitutional article mandates that all executive actions of the Government of India shall be expressed to be taken in the name of the President?**
   - A) Article 74
   - B) Article 75
   - C) Article 77
   - D) Article 76
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Article 77 deals with the conduct of business of the Government of India, stating actions are executed in the President's name. Why others are wrong: Articles 74 and 75 establish the Council of Ministers; Article 76 outlines the role of the Attorney General.

27. **What role does the Cabinet play regarding the summoning and proroguing of Parliament?**
   - A) It has no role as the President decides independently
   - B) It advises the President, whose advice is binding
   - C) It can dissolve Parliament without the President
   - D) It acts only if Parliament permits
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet decides when to session or end a parliamentary session and advises the President, who then issues the official orders. Why others are wrong: The President does not act independently in this regard; the Cabinet cannot bypass the executive head; Parliament cannot summon itself.

28. **Kitchen Cabinet refers to which type of body?**
   - A) A formal sub-committee listed in the constitution
   - B) An informal circle of trusted advisors to the Prime Minister
   - C) A committee dealing with food security and agriculture
   - D) A parliamentary committee on government assurances
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The 'Kitchen Cabinet' or 'Inner Cabinet' is an informal, extra-legal body consisting of the PM and 2-4 influential colleagues or friends. Why others are wrong: It has no constitutional validity; it does not deal with actual kitchens or food policies; it is not part of the parliamentary committee framework.

29. **Which body serves as the primary crisis management group in the Indian governmental structure?**
   - A) The Union Cabinet
   - B) The Supreme Court collegium
   - C) The Zonal Councils
   - D) The Inter-State Council
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Cabinet acts as the ultimate crisis manager of the nation, handling all major emergencies, internal security issues, and external threats. Why others are wrong: The collegium handles judicial appointments; Zonal and Inter-State councils focus on federal cooperation and coordination rather than immediate crisis management.

30. **When the Union Cabinet takes a major policy decision involving expenditure, which department ensures financial scrutiny before it reaches the floor?**
   - A) Department of Expenditure, Ministry of Finance
   - B) Comptroller and Auditor General
   - C) Reserve Bank of India
   - D) Securities and Exchange Board of India
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Department of Expenditure within the Ministry of Finance performs the preliminary financial appraisal before major financial policies hit the Cabinet. Why others are wrong: The CAG audits money after it is spent; the RBI manages monetary policy; SEBI regulates the securities market.

## National - Appointments (Core Constitutional Posts inside India) (National)

1.  Topic Introduction
    The given questions focus on Indian Polity, specifically covering Core Constitutional Posts inside India. This encompasses the highest executive, judicial, legislative, and administrative offices established directly by the Constitution of India, such as the President, Vice-President, Chief Justice, Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG), Attorney General, and various constitutional bodies. Understanding their appointments, tenures, powers, and constitutional articles forms a mandatory core segment of the General Awareness (Polity) syllabus for the SSC CGL exam.

2.  Core Concepts Explained Simply  - The President: The head of state, first citizen of India, and the formal executive authority of the Union. All executive actions are taken in their name.
  - The Vice-President: The second-highest constitutional office, serving as the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha and acting as President during vacancies.
  - Chief Justice of India (CJI): The head of the Indian judiciary and the Supreme Court, responsible for administering justice and presiding over constitutional matters.
  - Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG): The guardian of the public purse, responsible for auditing all receipts and expenditures of the Government of India and the states.
  - Attorney General for India: The chief legal advisor to the Government of India and the highest law officer in the country.
  - Constitutional vs. Non-Constitutional Bodies: Constitutional bodies are explicitly mentioned in the text of the Constitution (e.g., Finance Commission, Election Commission), while non-constitutional bodies are created by executive resolutions or acts of Parliament (e.g., NITI Aayog).3.  Important Facts and Dates  - Minimum Age for President/Vice-President: 35 years (Article 58 for President).
  - Key Constitutional Articles: Article 52 (President), Article 61 (Impeachment of President), Article 64 (VP as Chairman of Rajya Sabha), Article 72 (Pardoning powers), Article 74 (Council of Ministers), Article 76 (Attorney General), Article 110 (Money Bill certification), Article 123 (Ordinance making power), Article 124 (Supreme Court & CJI appointment), Article 148 (CAG), Article 155 (Governor appointment), Article 165 (Advocate General), Article 280 (Finance Commission), Article 324 (Election Commission), Article 352 (National Emergency).
  - CAG Tenure: 6 years or up to 65 years of age, whichever is earlier.
  - Supreme Court Judge Retirement Age: 65 years.4.  Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams  - Resignation Paths: The President submits their resignation to the Vice-President. The Prime Minister, CJI, and Governors submit theirs to the President. The Speaker of Lok Sabha resigns to the Deputy Speaker.
  - Special Powers of the Speaker: The Speaker of Lok Sabha holds the ultimate authority under Article 110(3) to decide whether a bill is a Money Bill or not, and their decision is final.
  - Finance Commission Frequency: Constituted by the President every 5 years under Article 280.
  - President Vacancy Succession Line: If both the President and Vice-President offices fall vacant, the Chief Justice of India acts as the President (under the President (Discharge of Functions) Act, 1969).5.  Differences Between Similar Terms  - Real Executive vs. Nominal Executive: The Prime Minister is the real executive head (head of government), whereas the President is the nominal executive head (head of state) in whom the executive power is constitutionally vested.
  - Attorney General vs. Advocate General: The Attorney General (Article 76) is the chief legal advisor at the Union level appointed by the President; the Advocate General (Article 165) is the chief legal advisor at the State level appointed by the Governor.
  - Adjournment vs. Dissolution: Adjournment terminates a sitting of the House and is done by the Speaker/Chairman; Dissolution ends the life of the Lok Sabha entirely and can only be done by the President (Article 85).6.  Examples  - Article 88 Participation: The Attorney General serves as a classic example of a non-member who has the right to speak and take part in the proceedings of either House of Parliament or committees, but does not have the right to vote.
  - Non-Constitutional Transition: NITI Aayog serves as the prime example of a non-constitutional, non-statutory body, which replaced the Planning Commission via an executive resolution.
  - Judicial Removal Process: A Supreme Court judge cannot be summarily dismissed; they can only be removed by the President after an address by Parliament passed with a special majority in both houses.7.  Previous-Year Exam Perspective
    SSC CGL exams consistently test exact Article numbers associated with core constitutional posts (e.g., matching Article 76 with Attorney General or Article 148 with CAG). Questions heavily target the appointing authorities (where the answer is almost always the President for central posts and the Governor for state posts), specific age qualifications (25 for Lok Sabha, 30 for Rajya Sabha, 35 for President), and the precise tenures or retirement ages of judicial and auditing heads.

8.  Quick Revision Points and Mnemonics  - Impeachment Article: Article 61 (Imagine "61" as the exit door for the President).
  - CAG Tenure: 6 years or 65 age (Remember the "6s": 6 years, 65 age, Article 148 -\> 1+4+8 = 13, which has no relation, so just memorize 6 and 65).
  - Legal Officers Articles: Union is 76 (Attorney General). Add 89 to get State level -\> 76 + 89 = 165 (Advocate General).
  - Money Bill Finality: Speaker of Lok Sabha speaks the final word on Money Bills.
  - Emergency Proclamation: President declares it, but only on the written advice of the Union Cabinet.

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **Who is the first citizen of India?**
   - A) Prime Minister
   - B) President
   - C) Chief Justice of India
   - D) Vice-President
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The President is considered the first citizen of India as the head of state and the highest constitutional authority.
Why others are wrong: The Prime Minister is the head of the government (not state); the Chief Justice is the head of the judiciary; the Vice-President is the second citizen of India.

2. **Who appoints the Chief Justice of India?**
   - A) Prime Minister
   - B) Parliament
   - C) President
   - D) Law Minister
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Under Article 124 of the Constitution, the President of India appoints the Chief Justice of India.
Why others are wrong: The Prime Minister and Law Minister can be part of the consultation process but do not possess the appointing authority; Parliament can only impeach judges, not appoint them.

3. **What is the minimum age required to contest the election for the President of India?**
   - A) 25 years
   - B) 30 years
   - C) 35 years
   - D) 40 years
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: According to Article 58 of the Indian Constitution, a person must have completed the age of 35 years to be eligible for election as President.
Why others are wrong: 25 years is the minimum age for Lok Sabha MPs; 30 years is the minimum age for Rajya Sabha MPs; 40 years is not a standard minimum age milestone in the Indian Constitution.

4. **Who serves as the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha?**
   - A) Speaker of Lok Sabha
   - B) President
   - C) Prime Minister
   - D) Vice-President
   - **Answer: D**
   - Explanation: Article 64 of the Constitution stipulates that the Vice-President of India shall be the ex-officio Chairman of the Council of States (Rajya Sabha).
Why others are wrong: The Speaker presides over the Lok Sabha; the President is the executive head and not part of daily parliamentary sessions; the Prime Minister is usually the Leader of the House in Lok Sabha.

5. **Which article of the Indian Constitution deals with the impeachment of the President?**
   - A) Article 52
   - B) Article 61
   - C) Article 72
   - D) Article 74
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Article 61 prescribes the detailed procedure for the impeachment of the President of India on the grounds of "violation of the Constitution".
Why others are wrong: Article 52 states there shall be a President; Article 72 deals with the pardoning powers of the President; Article 74 deals with the Council of Ministers advising the President.

6. **The Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) of India is appointed under which Article of the Constitution?**
   - A) Article 76
   - B) Article 110
   - C) Article 148
   - D) Article 280
   - **Answer: C**
