      - Island Dispute (China/Japan): Senkaku/Diaoyu Islands.
      - African Capital Shift: Yamoussoukro = Ivory Coast (CÃ´te d'Ivoire).
      - Arctic Resolution: Hans Island split = Canada and Denmark.

### Quick Practice (30 MCQs)

1. **Which country announced the shifting of its capital city from Jakarta to Nusantara due to environmental concerns?**
   - A) Malaysia
   - B) Indonesia
   - C) Philippines
   - D) Thailand
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Indonesia officially planned and began the process of shifting its capital from Jakarta to Nusantara on East Kalimantan island due to Jakarta sinking and facing severe traffic congestion. Why others are wrong: Malaysia's capital remains Kuala Lumpur; the Philippines' capital is Manila; Thailand's capital is Bangkok.

2. **The disputed region of Nagorno-Karabakh has been a major geopolitical hotspot primarily between which two countries?**
   - A) Russia and Ukraine
   - B) Armenia and Azerbaijan
   - C) Iran and Iraq
   - D) Kyrgyzstan and Tajikistan
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Nagorno-Karabakh is an enclave that has been the flashpoint of an intense, decades-long territorial and ethnic conflict between Armenia and Azerbaijan. Why others are wrong: Russia and Ukraine are locked in a different conflict; Iran and Iraq had historical border disputes but not over this region; Kyrgyzstan and Tajikistan have separate border skirmishes.

3. **The Kuril Islands dispute is a long-standing border issue between which two nations?**
   - A) China and Japan
   - B) Russia and Japan
   - C) South Korea and Japan
   - D) Russia and China
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Kuril Islands dispute involves the sovereignty over four islands seized by the Soviet Union at the end of WWII, which Japan claims as its Northern Territories. Why others are wrong: China and Japan dispute the Senkaku Islands; South Korea and Japan dispute the Dokdo/Takeshima islands; Russia and China resolved most of their border disputes in the 2000s.

4. **Which strategic maritime checkpoint, vital for global oil transit, sits as a geopolitical hotspot between Iran and the Arabian Peninsula?**
   - A) Strait of Malacca
   - B) Strait of Gibraltar
   - C) Strait of Hormuz
   - D) Bab-el-Mandeb
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The Strait of Hormuz is a crucial oil chokepoint controlled primarily by Iran on its northern coast, making it a constant geopolitical flashpoint. Why others are wrong: Strait of Malacca is in Southeast Asia; Strait of Gibraltar connects the Atlantic to the Mediterranean; Bab-el-Mandeb connects the Red Sea to the Gulf of Aden.

5. **In 2022, Egypt formally transferred sovereignty of two strategic Red Sea islands to Saudi Arabia after years of border deliberation. What are these islands?**
   - A) Socotra and Dahlak
   - B) Tiran and Sanafir
   - C) Kish and Qeshm
   - D) Zanzibar and Pemba
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The islands of Tiran and Sanafir, located at the mouth of the Gulf of Aqaba, were officially transferred from Egypt to Saudi Arabia. Why others are wrong: Socotra belongs to Yemen; Kish and Qeshm belong to Iran; Zanzibar and Pemba belong to Tanzania.

6. **The "Line of Actual Control" (LAC) is the disputed border demarcation between which two nuclear-armed powers?**
   - A) India and Pakistan
   - B) India and China
   - C) Pakistan and China
   - D) North Korea and South Korea
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The LAC serves as the effective border separating Indian-controlled territory from Chinese-controlled territory in the Himalayan region. Why others are wrong: India and Pakistan share the Line of Control (LoC); Pakistan and China have a settled border; the Koreas are separated by the DMZ.

7. **Which region voted overwhelmingly to switch its capital from Montserrat's de jure capital Plymouth (abandoned due to volcanic eruptions) to the temporary hub of Brades?**
   - A) Montserrat
   - B) Martinique
   - C) Guadeloupe
   - D) Saint Helena
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Montserrat's official capital Plymouth was buried by a volcanic eruption in 1997, forcing the de facto operations to shift to Brades in the north. Why others are wrong: Martinique, Guadeloupe, and Saint Helena did not abandon their capitals due to volcanic destruction in recent times.

8. **The Essequibo region is the subject of a major, renewed border and resource dispute between Venezuela and which neighboring country?**
   - A) Brazil
   - B) Colombia
   - C) Guyana
   - D) Suriname
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Venezuela claims sovereignty over the oil-rich Essequibo region, which comprises over two-thirds of the territory currently governed by Guyana. Why others are wrong: Brazil and Colombia share borders with Venezuela but are not disputing Essequibo; Suriname disputes a different border area with Guyana.

9. **Which European country recently experienced internal geopolitical tensions leading to discussions about moving administrative agencies to the eastern city of Debrecen?**
   - A) Poland
   - B) Hungary
   - C) Romania
   - D) Slovakia
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Hungary's government has initiated long-term plans to move several institutions and functions to its second-largest city, Debrecen, to balance development away from Budapest. Why others are wrong: Poland, Romania, and Slovakia have not announced structural shifts of major national organs to Debrecen.

10. **The Durand Line represents a highly contentious border issue and source of geopolitical friction between which two countries?**
   - A) India and Afghanistan
   - B) Pakistan and Afghanistan
   - C) Iran and Pakistan
   - D) Afghanistan and Uzbekistan
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Durand Line, established in 1893, is the international border between Pakistan and Afghanistan, which Afghan governments historically refuse to recognize formally. Why others are wrong: India does not share a de facto operating border with Afghanistan currently; Iran and Pakistan share a separate border; Afghanistan and Uzbekistan are separated by the Amu Darya river.

11. **The Senkaku Islands (known as Diaoyu Islands in another country) are a major hotspot of territorial tension between Japan and which nation?**
   - A) South Korea
   - B) Philippines
   - C) China
   - D) Vietnam
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The uninhabited Senkaku/Diaoyu islands in the East China Sea are claimed by both Japan and China, leading to regular naval standoffs. Why others are wrong: South Korea, the Philippines, and Vietnam have maritime disputes in other regions like the South China Sea or Sea of Japan, but not over the Senkakus.

12. **In 2019, which nation officially renamed its capital city from Astana to Nur-Sultan, only to revert the name back to Astana in 2022?**
   - A) Uzbekistan
   - B) Kazakhstan
   - C) Turkmenistan
   - D) Kyrgyzstan
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Kazakhstan renamed its capital city to Nur-Sultan in honor of its outgoing president, but restored the original name Astana three years later. Why others are wrong: Uzbekistan's capital is Tashkent; Turkmenistan's capital is Ashgabat; Kyrgyzstan's capital is Bishkek.

13. **The Chagos Archipelago is a long-running territorial sovereignty dispute in the Indian Ocean between the United Kingdom and which African nation?**
   - A) Seychelles
   - B) Madagascar
   - C) Mauritius
   - D) Maldives
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Mauritius claims sovereignty over the Chagos Archipelago, arguing that the UK illegally separated the islands from it prior to independence. Why others are wrong: Seychelles, Madagascar, and Maldives are Indian Ocean nations but are not the primary claimants in this dispute.

14. **The Hala'ib Triangle is a mineral-rich disputed border area located between which two North African countries?**
   - A) Libya and Egypt
   - B) Egypt and Sudan
   - C) Sudan and South Sudan
   - D) Morocco and Algeria
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Hala'ib Triangle is an area of land located on the African coast of the Red Sea, claimed by both Egypt and Sudan. Why others are wrong: Libya and Egypt have a defined western border; Sudan and South Sudan dispute Abyei; Morocco and Algeria have a closed land border dispute regarding Western Sahara.

15. **Which Baltic Sea hotspot, an exclave belonging to Russia, is a massive geopolitical point of friction surrounded by NATO members Poland and Lithuania?**
   - A) Kaliningrad
   - B) Crimea
   - C) Transnistria
   - D) Gotland
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Kaliningrad is a Russian exclave heavily fortified and sandwiched between Poland and Lithuania, creating intense regional geopolitical security issues. Why others are wrong: Crimea is on the Black Sea; Transnistria is a breakaway region in Moldova; Gotland belongs to Sweden.

16. **The Abyei Area remains a highly volatile border hotspot with unresolved administrative status between Sudan and which other nation?**
   - A) Chad
   - B) South Sudan
   - C) Ethiopia
   - D) Central African Republic
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Abyei region was left with a special administrative status following the 2011 independence of South Sudan, and both nations claim it. Why others are wrong: Chad, Ethiopia, and CAR border the Sudans but do not lay claim to the specific Abyei territory.

17. **In a symbolic move to assert administrative decentralization, Egypt has constructed a massive new hub to serve as the new capital. What is this project currently called?**
   - A) New Cairo City
   - B) New Administrative Capital
   - C) Neom
   - D) El Alamein
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Egypt has built the New Administrative Capital (NAC) to the east of Cairo to relieve pressure on the congested historical capital. Why others are wrong: New Cairo is an older suburb; Neom is a futuristic city project in Saudi Arabia; El Alamein is a northern coastal city.

18. **The Suwalki Gap is considered a highly vulnerable geopolitical chokepoint that connects Belarus with which heavily fortified Russian territory?**
   - A) Kaliningrad
   - B) St. Petersburg
   - C) Murmansk
   - D) Sevastopol
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The Suwalki Gap is a narrow corridor connecting Poland and Lithuania, which also separates the Russian exclave of Kaliningrad from Belarus. Why others are wrong: St. Petersburg, Murmansk, and Sevastopol are not located adjacent to the Suwalki corridor.

19. **The Aegean Dispute involves a series of interconnected border, airspace, and island militarization issues between which two NATO allies?**
   - A) Italy and Greece
   - B) Greece and Turkey
   - C) Turkey and Cyprus
   - D) Albania and Greece
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Greece and Turkey have long-standing sovereignty disputes over territorial waters, national airspace, and the status of islands in the Aegean Sea. Why others are wrong: Italy and Greece have settled maritime boundaries; Cyprus is not a member of NATO; Albania and Greece have minor maritime discussions but not the historic Aegean Dispute.

20. **Equatorial Guinea previously announced plans to move its official capital from Malabo to a newly constructed inland city named what?**
   - A) Oyala (Ciudad de la Paz)
   - B) Bata
   - C) EbebiyÃ­n
   - D) Mongomo
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Equatorial Guinea designed and built Ciudad de la Paz (formerly Oyala) in the jungle to replace Malabo as the national capital city. Why others are wrong: Bata is the largest city but not the planned new capital; EbebiyÃ­n and Mongomo are other regional towns.

21. **The Falkland Islands (Islas Malvinas) remain a significant post-colonial territorial flashpoint between the United Kingdom and which South American country?**
   - A) Chile
   - B) Brazil
   - C) Argentina
   - D) Uruguay
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Argentina claims sovereignty over the Falkland Islands, which led to a military conflict with the United Kingdom in 1982. Why others are wrong: Chile, Brazil, and Uruguay do not claim ownership of the Falkland Islands.

22. **Which body of water features overlapping territorial baselines and artificial island construction, turning it into a premier global geopolitical hotspot involving China, Vietnam, and the Philippines?**
   - A) East China Sea
   - B) South China Sea
   - C) Sea of Japan
   - D) Yellow Sea
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The South China Sea is subject to competing territorial claims by several nations over the Paracel and Spratly islands. Why others are wrong: East China Sea features the Senkaku dispute; Sea of Japan has the Dokdo dispute; Yellow Sea has fewer multi-national island building conflicts.

23. **The Preah Vihear Temple border dispute caused military skirmishes in the late 2000s and early 2010s between Cambodia and which neighboring state?**
   - A) Vietnam
   - B) Laos
   - C) Thailand
   - D) Myanmar
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: The International Court of Justice ruled on the sovereignty of the area surrounding the Preah Vihear Temple, which was disputed by Cambodia and Thailand. Why others are wrong: Vietnam, Laos, and Myanmar do not share the border sector containing the Preah Vihear Temple.

24. **What African nation officially moved its political capital from the coastal metropolis of Abidjan to the inland city of Yamoussoukro, though Abidjan remains the economic hub?**
   - A) Nigeria
   - B) Ivory Coast (CÃ´te d'Ivoire)
   - C) Ghana
   - D) Senegal
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Ivory Coast officially designated Yamoussoukro as its political capital in 1983, while keeping most offices and economic power in Abidjan. Why others are wrong: Nigeria moved its capital to Abuja; Ghana's capital is Accra; Senegal's capital is Dakar.

25. **The Ogaden region has historically been a significant geopolitical hotspot and border dispute zone between Ethiopia and which neighboring country?**
   - A) Eritrea
   - B) Somalia
   - C) Sudan
   - D) Kenya
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: The Ogaden region is an ethnic Somali area within Ethiopia that was the cause of the 1977-1978 Ogaden War between Somalia and Ethiopia. Why others are wrong: Eritrea fought a border war with Ethiopia over Badme; Sudan and Kenya have different border points with Ethiopia.

26. **The Golan Heights is a strategically vital plateau that represents a continuous geopolitical hotspot captured by Israel from which nation?**
   - A) Jordan
   - B) Lebanon
   - C) Syria
   - D) Egypt
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Israel captured the Golan Heights from Syria during the 1967 Six-Day War and later annexed it, a move not recognized by most of the international community. Why others are wrong: Jordan lost the West Bank; Lebanon shares a different disputed border (Shebaa Farms); Egypt lost and regained the Sinai Peninsula.

27. **South Korea and Japan maintain a persistent diplomatic dispute over the sovereignty of a pair of small islands known as Dokdo in Korea and what in Japan?**
   - A) Takeshima
   - B) Tsushima
   - C) Okinawa
   - D) Hokkaido
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: The islets are referred to as Dokdo in South Korea (which controls them) and Takeshima in Japan (which claims them). Why others are wrong: Tsushima is an undisputed Japanese island; Okinawa and Hokkaido are main prefectures/islands of Japan.

28. **In 1991, which country moved its federal capital away from the coastal city of Lagos to the centrally located planned city of Abuja?**
   - A) Kenya
   - B) Tanzania
   - C) Nigeria
   - D) South Africa
   - **Answer: C**
   - Explanation: Nigeria officially shifted its capital to Abuja in 1991 to have a neutral, centrally located capital city that reflects geographical and religious balance. Why others are wrong: Kenya's capital is Nairobi; Tanzania designated Dodoma to replace Dar es Salaam; South Africa has three capitals.

29. **The Fertile Triangle of the Amami Islands or the Migingo Island dispute represents a localized yet tense border issue on Lake Victoria between Kenya and which nation?**
   - A) Tanzania
   - B) Uganda
   - C) Rwanda
   - D) Burundi
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Migingo Island is a tiny, densely populated island on Lake Victoria that has caused minor diplomatic border friction between Kenya and Uganda. Why others are wrong: Tanzania shares Lake Victoria but is not part of the Migingo dispute; Rwanda and Burundi are landlocked and do not touch Lake Victoria.

30. **The Hans Island border dispute, humorously known as the "Whiskey War," was peacefully resolved in 2022 by dividing the island between Canada and which European nation's territory?**
   - A) United Kingdom
   - B) Denmark (Greenland)
   - C) Norway
   - D) Iceland
   - **Answer: B**
   - Explanation: Hans Island, located in the Arctic, was claimed by both Canada and Denmark (on behalf of Greenland) until they agreed to split the island in 2022. Why others are wrong: The UK, Norway, and Iceland were not claimants to Hans Island.
